diff --git a/home/stevepro/Steven/Code/testing2/sverx420/devkitSMS/PSGlib/README.md b/home/stevepro/Steven/Code/testing2/sverx420/devkitSMS/PSGlib/README.md index 0000000..0000000 100644 --- a/home/stevepro/Steven/Code/testing2/sverx420/devkitSMS/PSGlib/README.md +++ b/home/stevepro/Steven/Code/testing2/sverx420/devkitSMS/PSGlib/README.md @@ -1,67 +1,67 @@ PSGlib ====== These are all the currently defined functions/macros: ``` void PSGPlay (void *song); /* this will make your PSG tune start and loop forever */ void PSGPlayLoops (void *song, unsigned char loops); /* this will make your PSG tune start and loop the requested amount of times*/ void PSGPlayNoRepeat (void *song); /* this will make your PSG tune start and stop at loop */ void PSGCancelLoop (void); /* tell the library to stop the tune at next loop */ void PSGStop (void); /* this will make your PSG tune stop (pause) */ void PSGResume (void); /* this will make your stopped (paused) PSG tune resume playing */ unsigned char PSGGetStatus (void); /* get the current status of the tune */ PSGSetMusicVolumeChannelsAttenuation (chn0_attenuation,chn1_attenuation,chn2_attenuation,chn3_attenuation) /* (macro) this will set the tune volume attenuation (0-15) separately for each channel */ PSGSetMusicVolumeAttenuation (attenuation) /* (macro) this will set the tune volume attenuation (0-15) evenly for all the channels */ void PSGSetMusicVolumeChannelsAttenuation_f (unsigned int channels_attenuation); /* this is the underlying function for the above macros, and *NOT* meant to be called directly */ void PSGSFXPlay (unsigned char *sfx, unsigned char channels); /* this will make your SFX start */ void PSGSFXPlayLoop (unsigned char *sfx, unsigned char channels); /* this will make your looping SFX start */ void PSGSFXCancelLoop (void); /* tell the library to stop the SFX at next loop */ void PSGSFXStop (void); /* this will make your SFX stop */ unsigned char PSGSFXGetStatus (void); /* get the current status of the SFX */ void PSGSetSFXVolumeAttenuation (unsigned char attenuation); /* this will set the SFX volume attenuation (0-15) */ void PSGSilenceChannels (void); /* this will silence all PSG channels */ void PSGRestoreVolumes (void); /* this will restore PSG channels volume */ void PSGFrame (void); /* you should call this at a constant pace */ void PSGSFXFrame (void); /* you should call this too at a constant pace, if you use SFXs */ ``` Multibank support ----------------- If PSGlib is compiled with PSGLIB_MULTIBANK, the PSGPlay functions also take a bank argument. This initial bank is remembered by PSGlib and the bank will be switched automatically when calling PSGFrame(). PSGlib will automatically switch bank if the song data occupies more than 16kB, but it is assumed that the data is not segmented. (i.e. it must be consecutive in ROM) When PSGFrame() returns, the current bank in slot 2 may have been changed, so keep this in mind and take the necessary steps if necessary. (Set the bank again after the call, or use SMS_saveROMBank/restoreROMBank) When NOT compiled with PSGLIB_MULTIBANK, you must set the correct bank before calling PSGFrame manually and the song data cannot exceed 16kB. ``` /* Only if the library is compiled with PSGLIB_MULTIBANK. * Make sure to define PSGLIB_MULTIBANK before including * PSGlib.h */ void PSGPlay (void *song, unsigned char bank); /* this will make your PSG tune start and loop forever */ void PSGPlayLoops (void *song, unsigned char bank, unsigned char loops); /* this will make your PSG tune start and loop the requested amount of times*/ void PSGPlayNoRepeat (void *song, unsigned char bank); /* this will make your PSG tune start and stop at loop */ ```

handle IRQ jp _SMS_isr ;-------------------------------------------------------------------------- crt0_clear_ram: ld hl,#0xc000 ; save contents of $c000 ld a,(hl) ; before clearing RAM ld b,#0x00 ; clear RAM (to value 0x00) by setting ld (hl),b ; this is shameless ;-------------------------------------------------------------------------- .org 0x66 ; handle NMI jp _SMS_nmi_isr ;-------------------------------------------------------------------------- ; _OUTI96 label points to a block of 96 OUTI and a RET .rept 32 outi .endm _OUTI64:: ; _OUTI64 label points to a block of 64 OUTI and a RET .rept 32 outi .endm _OUTI32:: ; _OUTI32 label points to a block of 32 OUTI and a RET .rept 32 outi .endm _outi_block:: ; Created a separate [TransbotC project](/home/stevepro/Steven/Code/testing2/TransbotC) and built its [ROM](/home/stevepro/Steven/Code/testing2/TransbotC/build/transbot.sms).

The foundation translates the original graphics decoder and uses SMSlib to load the title assets. PSGlib is linked, but the original music format still needs conversion or a compatible player. The title is intended to remain on screen; gameplay and input are not yet implemented.

A clean build and decoder/asset tests passed. I haven’t verified the screen in an emulator, so visual fidelity remains to be checked. The original ROM and ASM output still have matching MD5 hashes.

See the [README](/home/stevepro/Steven/Code/testing2/TransbotC/README.md) for build instructions and the ASM-to-C mapping.

Yes — I think that’s a sensible approach for a faithful port. We can use [VanHalenDemoSMS](/home/stevepro/Steven/Code/testing2/VanHalenDemoSMS) as the organizational model while keeping Transbot’s existing engine and screen divisions. Its [sample manager](/home/stevepro/Steven/Code/testing2/VanHalenDemoSMS/dev/engine/sample_manager.c) already demonstrates C-callable inline Z80, so the idea fits your established style.

The important distinction is that this would initially be a hybrid C/assembly port, not yet a full C rewrite. That’s an advantage: we can preserve understood original behavior while translating and testing one subsystem at a time.

### What needs care

We can’t mechanically put every .inc inside a C function:

- Assembler syntax differs: Transbot uses WLA-DX; SDCC inline assembly uses its own assembler syntax.
- Calling conventions matter: many original routines exchange values in registers or jump into shared code rather than returning like C functions.
- Memory and interrupts are tightly coupled: the original uses fixed RAM addresses, its own reset and interrupt vectors, and raster timing. Those must be reconciled with SDCC’s startup and SMSlib, not simply combined.
- Original sound is custom: we can initially retain its assembly driver and data. PSGlib can replace it later, but the two should not independently control the PSG at the same time.
- ROM addresses matter: some original data contains absolute pointers, so moving it requires relocation or preserving its expected layout.

### Suggested structure

We could create a fresh project modeled on the demo, while keeping the useful test setup:

TransbotC/

  dev/

    main.c

    engine/

    object/

    screen/

    content/

  tests/

  Makefile

  README.md


I’d start with a small vertical slice: establish the build and module layout, then port startup and the title using carefully adapted assembly routines. Compare the result against the original in Emulicious before moving to input, interrupts, sound, and gameplay. SMSlib APIs can replace original routines where their behavior matches; timing-sensitive code can remain assembly until we understand it.

A matching MD5 is unlikely once SDCC and library startup are introduced, so the target would be matching behavior and presentation, validated against the original—not necessarily identical bytes. I wouldn’t replace the current project until we agree on the new baseline.

Great — glad it works!

make test runs two separate kinds of tests. Python coordinates them, but only one uses sz80:

1. Python unit tests — [test_export_assets.py](/home/stevepro/Steven/Code/testing2/TransbotCnew/tests/test_export_assets.py) tests the asset exporter’s validation logic directly in Python, including invalid and truncated streams. It does not use sz80.

2. Z80 decoder test — SDCC compiles [test_rle.c](/home/stevepro/Steven/Code/testing2/TransbotCnew/tests/test_rle.c) together with the actual inline assembly from [rle_manager.c](/home/stevepro/Steven/Code/testing2/TransbotCnew/dev/engine/rle_manager.c). The Python script [run_rle_test.py](/home/stevepro/Steven/Code/testing2/TransbotCnew/tests/run_rle_test.py) then launches sz80, SDCC’s Z80 CPU simulator.

The flow for the second test is:

C test + inline Z80

       ↓ SDCC

test_rle.ihx

       ↓ sz80 executes Z80 instructions

test result written to address $B000

       ↓ Python reads simulator output

pass or fail


The C test decodes each of the six original asset streams and compares the output hash with an expected value. Python finds the test’s end address in the linker map, runs the simulator to that point, and checks the result byte.

sz80 tests the CPU code, not the complete Master System display or sound hardware. Emulicious is still needed to check the actual screen and game behavior.

You can run both test groups with:

cd TransbotCnew

make test


The normal make build does not require sz80; it’s only needed for make test.
Conversation 5 · 07 Nov 2026 18:54

You

RLE stands for Runtime Length Encoding, correct? So what is it really here and why is it used here?

GitHub Copilot

Almost — RLE stands for Run-Length Encoding. It’s a simple form of compression: instead of storing a repeated byte many times, you store the byte and the number of times it repeats.

For example, conceptually:

Uncompressed:  00 00 00 00 00 00

Compressed:    repeat 00 six times


### What it means in Transbot

Transbot uses its own RLE format for graphics and some tilemap data. The decoder in [rle_manager.c](/home/stevepro/Steven/Code/testing2/TransbotCnew/dev/engine/rle_manager.c) understands two kinds of runs:

- Repeated run: a count followed by one byte to repeat.
- Literal run: a count followed by that many different bytes to copy.

A zero byte ends each stream. For example, a simplified single stream:

04 AA       → AA AA AA AA       repeat AA four times

83 10 20 30 → 10 20 30          copy three literal bytes

00          → end of stream


The high bit distinguishes literal runs from repeated runs; the lower seven bits give the count.

### Why the “lanes”?

The original decoder doesn’t write each stream contiguously. It writes one byte, skips ahead by two or four bytes, and then writes the next. It repeats that for each *lane*, producing interleaved output:

Lane 0:  A A B

Lane 1:  X Y Z



Output:  A X A Y B Z


This helps compress graphics because corresponding bytes can contain long runs, even if the final interleaved bytes do not. The decoded data is then uploaded to the Master System’s video memory with SMSlib.

### Why use it?

Primarily to save cartridge ROM space. Repeated patterns are common in graphics, and the Z80 can decompress this format without needing a complex decoder. The trade-off is that the game must spend time and RAM decompressing the data before displaying it.

The Python runner you tagged, [run_rle_test.py](/home/stevepro/Steven/Code/testing2/TransbotCnew/tests/run_rle_test.py), doesn’t perform the decompression itself: it starts sz80 to execute the actual Z80 decoder and checks the test result.

Read [](file:///home/stevepro/Steven/Code/testing2/TransbotCnew/README.md)
diff --git a/home/stevepro/Steven/Code/testing2/TransbotCnew/README.md b/home/stevepro/Steven/Code/testing2/TransbotCnew/README.md index 0000000..0000000 100644 --- a/home/stevepro/Steven/Code/testing2/TransbotCnew/README.md +++ b/home/stevepro/Steven/Code/testing2/TransbotCnew/README.md @@ -1,50 +1,50 @@ # Transbot hybrid C / Z80 port This is a new, independent vertical slice; `../TransbotC` and `../TransbotASM` are not modified. The `dev/engine` and `dev/screen` layout follows the style of `../VanHalenDemoSMS`. The goal is to preserve original behavior with C-callable assembly first, then replace routines with C/SMSlib selectively. ## Build and test Requires SDCC 4.2.0 with `ihx2sms` and `sz80`, Python 3 and make, plus the included `../sverx420/devkitSMS` libraries. Run: ```sh cd TransbotCnew make test make ``` Open `build/transbot.sms` in Emulicious or another SMS emulator. `make clean` removes generated build files. The build extracts title assets from `../TransbotASM/TransBot.sms` after checking its MD5. Do not distribute the generated ROM or assets unless you have the necessary rights. `make test` executes the actual SDCC-assembled decoder in the `sz80` Z80 simulator and checks independently recorded hashes for all six asset streams. The exporter also validates stream boundaries and decoded buffer sizes before the unbounded original-style assembly is allowed to consume them. ## Slice and correspondence | Original | This project | | --- | --- | | `engine/vdp_manager.inc`, `$291A` | `dev/engine/rle_manager.c`: Z80 instructions adapted to SDCC syntax, with C-callable 2- and 4-lane entry points. The wrapper preserves IX and uses a relocatable buffer instead of fixed `$D000`. | | `engine/boot_manager.inc`, `$2902`, `$28F0`, `$28F6`, `$28FC` | `dev/engine/content_manager.c`: original boot graphics decoded by the assembly routine and uploaded with SMSlib. | | `screen/title_screen.inc`, `engine/text_manager.inc` `$255E` | `dev/screen/title_screen.c` and content manager: palette, title graphic and fixed text. | | Reset/IRQ/NMI vectors | devkitSMS CRT and SMSlib for now, not the original vector handlers. | | Original sound engine | Not ported. PSGlib is linked and silenced; no song is played. Original song bytes are not PSGlib format. | The title map's second RLE lane continues into the following original ROM data before its terminator. The extractor intentionally includes those bytes, validates both lanes, and the assembly reproduces the complete decode. Only the first 68 words are copied to the screen, as in the original. This is **not playable**: title timing, score digits, controller transitions, demo/gameplay, original sound, reset/pause semantics, and raster IRQ behavior remain to be ported. It holds the title indefinitely. Library startup and interrupt timing differ from the original, so the resulting ROM is not expected to match its MD5. Compare display, timing, input and sound against the original in an emulator as later slices are added.

Do not edit. */", ""] for name, data in assets: lines.append(f"static const unsigned char {name}[] = {{") for offset in range(0, len(data), 16): lines.append(" " + ", ".join(f"0x{value:02x}" for value in data[offset:offset + 16]) + ",") lines.extend(("};", "")) output = Path(sys.argv[2]) temporary = None try: with tempfile.NamedTemporaryFile(mode="w", dir=output.parent, delete=False) as file: temporary = file.name file.write("\n".join(lines)) os.replace(temporary, output) finally: if temporary and os.path.exists(temporary): os.unlink(temporary) if __name__ == "__main__": main()

start=0x7a80 pairs=269 frames=1516 terminator=0x7c9a byte=0x0 first pairs: 0c 00 0d 08 0b 09 02 08 04 0a 01 02 0f 06 01 26
It comes from tracing the original disassembly and ROM data—not from the label alone.

### Why $2519 is recorded-input playback

In [input_manager.inc](/home/stevepro/Steven/Code/testing2/TransbotASM/engine/input_manager.inc:2), the input update checks a flag and, when bit 3 is set, jumps to [_LABEL_2519_](/home/stevepro/Steven/Code/testing2/TransbotASM/engine/input_manager.inc:149) instead of reading the controller ports.

The demo setup in [game_screen.inc](/home/stevepro/Steven/Code/testing2/TransbotASM/screen/game_screen.inc:19) sets that flag and copies five initial state bytes into RAM. Those bytes are in [vector_manager.inc](/home/stevepro/Steven/Code/testing2/TransbotASM/engine/vector_manager.inc:51):

.db $02 $00 $00 $80 $7A


The last two bytes form the little-endian pointer $7A80. At $2519, the routine reads from that pointer and writes the input value to _RAM_C10C_, the same input location used for the player.

### Why 1,516 frames

The data at $7A80 is a sequence of duration, input byte pairs, ending with a zero duration. The routine applies each input for the specified number of updates. Its first pairs are:

0C 00   → input $00 for 12 updates

0D 08   → input $08 for 13 updates

0B 09   → input $09 for 11 updates

02 08   → input $08 for 2 updates


I summed all the duration bytes up to the terminator at $7C9A: 269 pairs, totaling 1,516 input updates. The update routine is called from the VBlank handler, so those correspond to 1,516 VBlank frames during uninterrupted playback.

The test in [test_demo.c](/home/stevepro/Steven/Code/testing2/TransbotCnew/tests/test_demo.c) checks that full sequence, then checks that the following update detects the terminator. It tests the input stream—not yet the rendered gameplay.

#!/usr/bin/env python3 """Extract and validate the specific original ROM streams used by the port.""" import hashlib import os from pathlib import Path import sys import tempfile EXPECTED_MD5 = "dd26c323d1323691fed04cd8498afbcf" BUFFER_SIZE = 0x0C00 STREAMS = ( ("boot_font", 0x4957, 0x5116, (768, 768, 768, 768)), ("boot_tiles_a", 0x58F6, 0x6455, (1536, 1536)), ("boot_tiles_b", 0x6455, 0x6E4E, (1280, 1280)), ("boot_tiles_c", 0x6E4E, 0x76EF, (1280, 1280)), ("title_tiles", 0x3F4F, 0x430A, (416, 416, 416, 416)), ("title_map", 0x430A, 0x436B, (68, 1274)), ) def validate_stream(data, lengths): pos = 0 lanes = len(lengths) for lane, expected in enumerate(lengths): written = 0 while True: if pos >= len(data): raise ValueError("missing lane terminator") command = data[pos] pos += 1 if command == 0: break count = command & 0x7F if count == 0: raise ValueError("invalid zero-length run") payload = count if command & 0x80 else 1 if pos + payload > len(data): raise ValueError("truncated run") pos += payload written += count if (written - 1) * lanes + lane >= BUFFER_SIZE: raise ValueError("stream exceeds decoder buffer") if written != expected: raise ValueError(f"lane {lane} has {written} bytes, expected {expected}") if pos != len(data): raise ValueError("unexpected data after final lane") def validate_text(data): pos = 1 if not data or data[0] != 5: raise ValueError("invalid title text count") for _ in range(data[0]): if pos + 4 > len(data): raise ValueError("truncated title text header") address = int.from_bytes(data[pos:pos + 2], "little") size = int.from_bytes(data[pos + 2:pos + 4], "little") pos += 4

def lanes(d,s,e,n):
    pos=s;res=[]
    for l in range(n):
        w=0
        while True:
            c=d[pos];pos+=1
            if c==0:break
            k=c&0x7f
            pos+= k if c&0x80 else 1
            w+=k
        res.append(w)
    return res,pos,e

cd /home/stevepro/Steven/Code/testing2/TransbotCnew; python3 - <<'E'
d=open('../TransbotASM/TransBot.sms','rb').read()
def dec(s,n):
    pos=s;buf={}
    for l in range(n):
        i=0
        while True:
            c=d[pos];pos+=1
            if c==0:break
            k=c&0x7f
            for j in range(k):
                buf[(i+j)*n+l]=d[pos+(j if c&0x80 else 0)]
            pos+= k if c&0x80 else 1
            i+=k
    return bytes(buf[i] for i in range(len(buf)))
def fnv(b):
    h=2166136261
    for x in b: h=((h^x)*16777619)&0xffffffff
    return hex(h),len(b)
print(fnv(dec(0x5116,4)),fnv(dec(0x4446,2)), fnv(dec(0x4957,4)))

Edit [](file:///home/stevepro/Steven/Code/testing2/TransbotCnew/README.md)
The stage-loading half of gameplay is in, but the game screen is still blank. I haven't run the ROM in an emulator, so that part is untested. The ROM builds (make test all passes) and the new RLE checks pass in sz80.

What I added to TransbotCnew
- Stage loading: engine_content_manager_load_stage() in the content manager loads the palette, then decodes and uploads the stage tiles from $5116 to VRAM 0x2C00. It also decodes the stage map from $4446 into the decode buffer, where the scroll code will read it.
- Game screen: screen_game_screen_load() in the new dev/screen/game_screen.{c,h} calls the loader and clears the name table and sprites. Press button 1 or 2 on the title screen to reach it. The BG palette is loaded but nothing is drawn yet, so it shows blank.
- Exporter and tests: export_assets.py now extracts and validates the two new streams. test_rle.c checks both decoded streams against hashes from an independent Python decoder. The README is updated.

The $0429 setup copies 0x1240 bytes to VRAM, but only the first 0xC00 are real stage tiles. The rest is stale buffer data that the original overwrites immediately with the cleared name table, so I skipped it.

Why nothing is drawn yet: the background is built by the scroll engine at $2957, $29FA and $2C95. It streams meta-tile columns into RAM buffers at fixed addresses ($C112–$C12E, $D000–$D8xx). The object, collision and map code also uses fixed addresses from $C100 to $C61F and $DE00.

Next step: I plan to put the original RAM layout at those fixed addresses and move the SDCC data out of the way. Then I'd port the scroll engine ($2957 → $2C95/$29FA) so the first stage background appears. Audio stays deferred, as you chose.

Your NOTES.md only holds scratch notes about the sz80 test approach, so nothing in it changed the plan. TransbotC and TransbotCnew01 are untouched.

Should I go ahead with the RAM layout and scroll engine port?

cat > dev/engine/original_ram.h <<'E'
#ifndef ORIGINAL_RAM_H
#define ORIGINAL_RAM_H

/*
 * The ported original routines address fixed RAM ($C000-$C61F, decode buffer at
 * $D000). The link places SDCC's own variables at $C620 so the two never overlap.
 */
#define ORIGINAL_RAM_START 0xc100u
#define ORIGINAL_RAM_SIZE  0x0520u

#define ORIG_BYTE(address) (*(volatile unsigned char *)(address))
#define ORIG_WORD(address) (*(volatile unsigned int *)(address))

#define ORIG_SCROLL_X_BOTTOM ORIG_BYTE(0xc145)
#define ORIG_SCROLL_X_TOP    ORIG_BYTE(0xc14f)
#define ORIG_SCROLL_FLAGS    ORIG_BYTE(0xc152)  /* bit3 scrolling, bit5 bonus, bit7 redraw */
#define ORIG_SCROLL_ROW_PTR  ORIG_WORD(0xc153)
#define ORIG_STAGE_STATE     ORIG_BYTE(0xc16e)
#define ORIG_ROW_INDEX       ORIG_BYTE(0xc15a)
#define ORIG_ROW_INDEX_2     ORIG_BYTE(0xc15c)

#define SCROLL_FLAG_ACTIVE 0x08
#define SCROLL_FLAG_BONUS  0x20

#endif
E
cat > dev/engine/scroll_manager.h <<'E'
#ifndef SCROLL_MANAGER_H
#define SCROLL_MANAGER_H

/* Meta-tile row sequence for the first stage ($2B4D in the original ROM). */
extern const unsigned char engine_scroll_sequence[];

/* $2957: per-frame scroll state machine (main loop). */
void engine_scroll_prepare(void);
/* VBlank half of the $0FA6 handler: name-table streaming, then $29FA scrolling. */
void engine_scroll_frame(void);

#endif
E
cat > dev/engine/scroll_manager.c <<'E'
#include "scroll_manager.h"

/* Entry points of the byte-exact conversion in scroll_manager_z80.s. */
void engine_scroll_prepare(void) __naked
{
    __asm
        push ix
        push iy
        call _engine_scroll_prepare_z80
        pop iy
        pop ix
        ret
    __endasm;
}

void engine_scroll_frame(void) __naked
{
    __asm
        push ix
        push iy
        call _engine_scroll_draw_columns
        call _engine_scroll_advance
        pop iy
        pop ix
        ret
    __endasm;
}
E

cat > dev/screen/game_screen.c <<'E'
#include <string.h>
#include "SMSlib.h"
#include "../engine/content_manager.h"
#include "../engine/original_ram.h"
#include "../engine/scroll_manager.h"
#include "game_screen.h"

/* VBlank part of $0FA6: top-region scroll, line counter, then the ported scroll engine. */
static void game_frame_interrupt(void)
{
    INLINE_SMS_setBGScrollX(ORIG_SCROLL_X_TOP);
    SMS_VDPControlPort = 0x7f;
    SMS_VDPControlPort = 0x8a;
    engine_scroll_frame();
}

/* Line-interrupt part of $0FA6: scroll for the lower region of the screen. */
static void game_line_interrupt(void)
{
    if (ORIG_SCROLL_FLAGS & SCROLL_FLAG_BONUS)
        return;
    INLINE_SMS_setBGScrollX(ORIG_SCROLL_X_BOTTOM);
    SMS_VDPControlPort = 0xff;
    SMS_VDPControlPort = 0x8a;
}

/* Scroll-related part of $0429 and the stage-1 start of $03B2; objects are not ported yet. */
void screen_game_screen_load(void)
{
    SMS_displayOff();
    SMS_setFrameInterruptHandler(0);
    SMS_setLineInterruptHandler(0);
    memset((void *)ORIGINAL_RAM_START, 0, ORIGINAL_RAM_SIZE);

    engine_content_manager_load_stage();
    SMS_VRAMmemsetW(0x3800, 0x1100, 0x0700);
    SMS_initSprites();
    SMS_copySpritestoSAT();

    ORIG_SCROLL_ROW_PTR = (unsigned int)engine_scroll_sequence;
    ORIG_ROW_INDEX = 0xff;
    ORIG_ROW_INDEX_2 = 0xff;
    ORIG_STAGE_STATE = 1;
    engine_scroll_prepare();
    ORIG_SCROLL_FLAGS |= SCROLL_FLAG_ACTIVE;

    SMS_setLineCounter(0x7f);
    SMS_setFrameInterruptHandler(game_frame_interrupt);
    SMS_setLineInterruptHandler(game_line_interrupt);
    SMS_enableLineInterrupt();
    SMS_displayOn();
}

void screen_game_screen_update(void)
{
    engine_scroll_prepare();
}
E

Terminal output
ihx2sms build/transbot.ihx build/transbot.sms *** sverx's ihx2sms converter *** Info: 19143 bytes used/32768 total [58.42%] - size of output ROM is 32 KB Info: SEGA header found, checksum updated
I've done the RAM layout and scroll engine port. The stage-1 desert background now draws and scrolls in Emulicious. I checked this with an auto-start build of the same code, in a scratch folder I've since deleted; I didn't test pressing a button on the title screen to enter the game. A clean make test all passes.

RAM layout
- The link now uses --data-loc 0xC620, so SMSlib and SDCC variables sit above the original's fixed $C000–$C61F block. I confirmed this in the map file.
- engine_rle_buffer is fixed at $D000, where the original scroll tables expect it.
- original_ram.h names the original variables that the C code touches.

Scroll engine
- Converter: tools/port_asm.py converts the original WLA routines to SDCC assembler. These are the scroll manager, the $2D01–$2D6F block, the VDP helpers and the VBlank column streaming.
- Verification: make test now assembles the converted code at the original addresses and requires all 1,228 bytes to equal the ROM. It also checks that the generated file [scroll_manager_z80.s](/home/stevepro/Steven/Code/testing2/TransbotCnew/dev/engine/scroll_manager_z80.s) is up to date.
- ASM quirk: the original writes ld hl,_DATA_8_ and ld de,_DATA_F_ to mean the numbers 8 and 15. The converter emits plain numbers for those. It's noted in the README.
- Interrupts: [game_screen.c](/home/stevepro/Steven/Code/testing2/TransbotCnew/dev/screen/game_screen.c) ports the $0FA6 frame and line scroll-register writes to SMSlib's frame and line interrupt hooks. The line counter is $7F, as in the original.
- Entering the game: pressing button 1 or 2 on the title now starts this scrolling background. The main loop in main.c calls the scroll update each frame afterwards.

Not ported yet:
- Objects, the player and HUD.
- Collision and input.
- The stage sequencer at $50A/$94A, which normally switches scrolling on. For now screen_game_screen_load() switches it on directly.
- Sound.

TransbotC and TransbotCnew01 are untouched. I noticed a TransbotCnew02 folder that I didn't create.

Next I'd port the object system, starting with the player object, its sprite and the input, so something moves on the scrolling stage. Shall I proceed?

PS: in the build folder - why do we have assets.h

GitHub Copilot

assets.h is generated at build time by tools/export_assets.py, which reads the ROM and checks its MD5 first. It holds the decoded-source graphics and text as C arrays. It lives in build/ and is git-ignored, so no ROM-derived data ends up in source control. Now I'll look at the object system.

#ifndef ORIGINAL_GAME_H
#define ORIGINAL_GAME_H

/*
 * Symbols of the original Z80 game code, converted by tools/port_asm.py into
 * build/original_game.s. Every routine takes no arguments and may clobber all
 * registers; the wrappers preserve IX/IY for the SDCC ABI. The graphics data
 * lives in ROM bank 2, which must stay mapped in slot 2 (see main.c).
 */
extern const unsigned char boot_font[];
extern const unsigned char boot_tiles_a[];
extern const unsigned char boot_tiles_b[];
extern const unsigned char boot_tiles_c[];
extern const unsigned char title_tiles[];
extern const unsigned char title_map[];
extern const unsigned char title_text[];
extern const unsigned char stage_tiles[];
extern const unsigned char stage_map[];

void orig_screen_setup(void);    /* $0429 */
void orig_stage_start(void);     /* $06A9 */
void orig_object_update(void);   /* $0123 */
void orig_stage_sequencer(void); /* $050A */
void orig_scroll_prepare(void);  /* $2957 */
void orig_map_trigger(void);     /* $0817 */
void orig_map_next(void);        /* $0835 */
void orig_spawn_pending(void);   /* $097B */

void orig_vblank_columns(void);  /* name-table streaming from the $0FA6 handler */
void orig_scroll_advance(void);  /* $29FA */
void orig_input_update(void);    /* $2433 */
void orig_player_update(void);   /* $0AEC */
void orig_timer_update(void);    /* $266F */
void orig_score_update(void);    /* $2DFD */
void orig_hud_update(void);      /* $2E3E */
void orig_stage_timer(void);     /* $0934 */

#endif

1) * lanes + lane >= BUFFER_SIZE: raise ValueError("stream exceeds decoder buffer") if written != expected: raise ValueError(f"lane {lane} has {written} bytes, expected {expected}") if pos != len(data): raise ValueError("unexpected data after final lane") def validate_text(data): pos = 1 if not data or data[0] != 5: raise ValueError("invalid title text count") for _ in range(data[0]): if pos + 4 > len(data): raise ValueError("truncated title text header") address = int.from_bytes(data[pos:pos + 2], "little") size = int.from_bytes(data[pos + 2:pos + 4], "little") pos += 4 if address < 0x3800 or address + size > 0x3F00 or pos + size > len(data): raise ValueError("invalid title text bounds") pos += size if pos != len(data): raise ValueError("unexpected trailing title text") def validate_demo(data): if len(data) != 539 or data[-1] != 0: raise ValueError("incorrect demo stream length or terminator") for pos in range(0, len(data) - 1, 2): if not data[pos] or data[pos + 1] & 0xC0: raise ValueError(f"invalid demo input pair at offset {pos}") def main(): if len(sys.argv) not in (3, 4) or (len(sys.argv) == 4 and sys.argv[3] != "--demo"): raise SystemExit("usage: export_assets.py ROM OUTPUT [--demo]") rom = Path(sys.argv[1]).read_bytes() actual = hashlib.md5(rom).hexdigest() if actual != EXPECTED_MD5: raise SystemExit(f"Unexpected ROM checksum: {actual} (expected {EXPECTED_MD5})") assets = [] if len(sys.argv) == 4: demo = rom[0x7A80:0x7C9B] validate_demo(demo) assets.append(("demo_input", demo)) else: for name, start, end, lengths in STREAMS: data = rom[start:end] try: validate_stream(data, lengths) except ValueError as error: raise SystemExit(f"Invalid {name}: {error}") from error assets.append((name, data)) text = rom[0x2584:0x264B] validate_text(text) assets.append(("title_text", text)) lines = ["/* Generated from the verified original ROM. cd /home/stevepro/Steven/Code/testing2/TransbotCnew; cat > dev/screen/game_screen.c <<'E'
#include <string.h>
#include "SMSlib.h"
#include "../engine/original_game.h"
#include "../engine/original_ram.h"
#include "game_screen.h"

static void game_frame_body(void);

/*
 * VBlank part of $0FA6. SMSlib's handler preserves AF/BC/DE/HL/IX/IY, but the
 * original code also uses the alternate register set, so this wrapper saves it.
 */
static void game_frame_interrupt(void) __naked
{
    __asm
        ex af, af'
        push af
        ex af, af'
        exx
        push bc
        push de
        push hl
        exx
        call _game_frame_body
        exx
        pop hl
        pop de
        pop bc
        exx
        ex af, af'
        pop af
        ex af, af'
        ret
    __endasm;
}

static void game_frame_body(void)
{
    INLINE_SMS_setBGScrollX(ORIG_SCROLL_X_TOP);
    SMS_VDPControlPort = 0x7f;
    SMS_VDPControlPort = 0x8a;

    if (ORIG_FRAME_COUNTER < 0x0f)
        ++ORIG_FRAME_COUNTER;
    if (ORIG_FRAME_READY)
        return;

    if (ORIG_SAT_FLAGS & 0x80) {
        SMS_VRAMmemcpy(0x3f00, (const void *)0xc240, 0x40);
        SMS_VRAMmemcpy(0x3f80, (const void *)0xc280, 0x80);
        ORIG_SAT_FLAGS &= 0x7f;
    }
    orig_vblank_columns();
    orig_scroll_advance();
    orig_input_update();
    orig_player_update();
    orig_timer_update();
    orig_score_update();
    orig_hud_update();
    orig_stage_timer();
    ORIG_FRAME_READY = 1;
}

/* Line-interrupt part of $0FA6: scroll for the lower region of the screen. */
static void game_line_interrupt(void)
{
    if (ORIG_SCROLL_FLAGS & SCROLL_FLAG_BONUS)
        return;
    INLINE_SMS_setBGScrollX(ORIG_SCROLL_X_BOTTOM);
    SMS_VDPControlPort = 0xff;
    SMS_VDPControlPort = 0x8a;
}

/* Gameplay start of the original state 4 ($03B2): stage 1, one player. */
void screen_game_screen_load(void)
{
    SMS_displayOff();
    SMS_setFrameInterruptHandler(0);
    SMS_setLineInterruptHandler(0);

    memset((void *)ORIGINAL_RAM_START, 0, ORIGINAL_RAM_SIZE);
    ORIG_FRAME_READY = 0;
    ORIG_FRAME_COUNTER = 0;
    ORIG_PAD_DETECT = 0xff;
    ORIG_MODE_FLAGS = 0x50;
    ORIG_SAT_BUFFER_FLAGS = 0;

    orig_screen_setup();
    orig_stage_start();
    ORIG_STAGE_STATE = 1;

    SMS_setLineCounter(0x7f);
    SMS_setFrameInterruptHandler(game_frame_interrupt);
    SMS_setLineInterruptHandler(game_line_interrupt);
    SMS_enableLineInterrupt();
    SMS_displayOn();
}

/* Main-loop part of the original game state: runs once per completed VBlank. */
void screen_game_screen_update(void)
{
    if (!ORIG_FRAME_READY)
        return;
    ORIG_FRAME_READY = 0;
    orig_object_update();
    orig_stage_sequencer();
    orig_scroll_prepare();
    orig_map_trigger();
    orig_map_next();
    orig_spawn_pending();
}

GAMEPLAY_FILES = ( ("engine/vector_manager.inc", 5, 21), # palette and constants read as data ("engine/vector_manager.inc", 26, 30), # VDP register init table "object/object_manager.inc", "engine/screen_manager.inc", "engine/stage_manager.inc", "engine/map_manager.inc", "object/sprite_object.inc", "object/player_object.inc", "engine/pool_manager.inc", "engine/collision_manager.inc", ("engine/interrupt_manager.inc", 143, 154), # tables read by collision code ("engine/interrupt_manager.inc", 109, 141, "_orig_vblank_columns"), "object/motion_object.inc", "object/spawn_object.inc", "object/enemy_object.inc", "engine/input_manager.inc", "engine/text_manager.inc", "engine/vdp_manager.inc", "engine/scroll_manager.inc", "engine/score_manager.inc", "object/behavior_table.inc", "content/sprite_data.inc", "content/gfx_data.inc", "content/level_data.inc", ) # Files whose data is placed in switchable ROM bank 2 (always mapped at $8000). LITERAL_LABELS = {"_DATA_8_": 8, "_DATA_F_": 15, "_DATA_20_": 32} REGS = {"a", "b", "c", "d", "e", "h", "l", "af", "bc", "de", "hl", "sp", "ix", "iy", "af'", "i", "r"} CONDS = {"z", "nz", "c", "nc", "po", "pe", "p", "m"} ARITH1 = {"sub", "and", "or", "xor", "cp"} BRANCH = {"jp", "jr", "call", "djnz"} JR_OPCODES = {"nz": 0x20, "z": 0x28, "nc": 0x30, "c": 0x38} LABEL = r"(?:_(?:LABEL|DATA)_[0-9A-F]+_)" class Converter: def __init__(self, relocatable): self.relocatable = relocatable self.counts = {} self.defined = set() self.used = set() self.wrappers = [] def anon(self, token, define): family = token[0] key = family * len(token) if define: self.counts[key] = self.counts.get(key, 0) + 1 return f"al_{'m' if family == '-' else 'p'}{len(token)}_{self.counts[key]}" n = self.counts.get(key, 0) + (1 if family == "+" else 0) return f"al_{'m' if family == '-' else 'p'}{len(token)}_{n}" def expr(self, text): text = text.strip() if re.fullmatch(r"[+-]+", text): return self.anon(text, False) def name(match): word = match.group(0) if word in PORTS: return f"0x{PORTS[word]:02X}" if self.relocatable and word in LITERAL_LABELS: return str(LITERAL_LABELS[word]) ram = re.fullmatch(r"_RAM_([0-9A-F]{4})_", word) if ram: return f"0x{ram.group(1)}" self.used.add(word) return word text = re.sub(r"\$([0-9A-Fa-f]+)", lambda m: f"0x{m.group(1)}", text) text = re.sub(r"%([01]+)", lambda m: f"0b{m.group(1)}", text) return re.sub(r"_(?:RAM|LABEL|DATA)_[0-9A-F]+_|_PORT_[0-9A-F]+_|Port_\w+", name, text) def operand(self, mnemonic, op, index, count): op = op.strip() low = op.lower() if low in REGS: return low if mnemonic in BRANCH | {"ret"} and index == 0 and count == 2 and low in CONDS: return low if mnemonic == "ret" and low in CONDS: return low if mnemonic in {"bit", "set", "res", "im", "rst"} and index == 0: return self.expr(op) if mnemonic in {"im", "rst"} else op index_match = re.fullmatch(r"\((ix|iy)\s*([+-])\s*([^)]+)\)", low) if index_match: reg, sign, disp = index_match.groups() disp = self.expr(disp) return f"{disp if sign == '+' else '-' + disp}({reg})" if low in ("(ix)", "(iy)"): return f"0{low}" if op.startswith("(") and op.endswith(")"): inner = op[1:-1].strip() if inner.lower() in {"hl", "de", "bc", "sp", "c"}: return f"({inner.lower()})" return f"({self.expr(inner)})" if mnemonic in BRANCH: return self.expr(op) return "#" + self.expr(op) def instruction(self, text): parts = text.split(None, 1) mnemonic = parts[0].lower() operands = [o.strip() for o in parts[1].split(",")] if len(parts) > 1 else [] if mnemonic == "jr" and operands[-1].isdigit(): # The ROM jumps one byte into the middle of the next instruction. opcode = 0x18 if len(operands) == 1 else JR_OPCODES[operands[0].lower()] return f"\t.db 0x{opcode:02X},{operands[-1]}" if mnemonic in ARITH1 and len(operands) == 1: operands.insert(0, "a") converted = [self.operand(mnemonic, o, i, len(operands)) for i, o in enumerate(operands)] return f"\t{mnemonic}" + (" " + ",".join(converted) if converted else "") def line(self, raw, data_dir): text = raw.split(";", 1)[0].strip() if not text: return None label = re.fullmatch(rf"({LABEL}|[+-]+):(.*)", text) if label: token, rest = label.groups() if re.fullmatch(r"[+-]+", token): name = self.anon(token, True) else: name = token self.defined.add(token) out = [name + ":"] if self.relocatable and token in ALIASES: out.append(ALIASES[token] + "::") if self.relocatable and token in EXPORTS: self.wrappers.append((EXPORTS[token], token)) if rest.strip(): out.append(self.line(rest, data_dir)) return "\n".join(out) if text.startswith(".db"): return "\t.db " + ",".join(self.expr(v) for v in text[3:].split()) if text.startswith(".dw"): return "\t.dw " + ",".join(self.expr(v) for v in text[3:].split()) dsb = re.fullmatch(r"\.dsb\s+(\d+)\s*,\s*(\S+)", text) if dsb: return "\t.db " + ",".join([self.expr(dsb.group(2))] * int(dsb.group(1))) incbin = re.fullmatch(r'\.

diff --git a/home/stevepro/Steven/Code/testing2/TransbotCnew/tests/run_rle_test.py b/home/stevepro/Steven/Code/testing2/TransbotCnew/tests/run_rle_test.py index 0000000..0000000 100644 --- a/home/stevepro/Steven/Code/testing2/TransbotCnew/tests/run_rle_test.py +++ b/home/stevepro/Steven/Code/testing2/TransbotCnew/tests/run_rle_test.py @@ -1,40 +1,40 @@ #!/usr/bin/env python3 """Run the compiled Z80 decoder tests in the SDCC simulator.""" from pathlib import Path import re import subprocess import sys def run_test(simulator, ihx, map_file, name): map_text = Path(map_file).read_text() match = re.search(r"^\s*([0-9A-Fa-f]+)\s+_test_done\s", map_text, re.MULTILINE) if not match: raise SystemExit("Cannot find test_done in linker map") address = int(match.group(1), 16) commands = f"run 0 0x{address:x}\ndump /x rom 0xe000 0xb000\nquit\n" result = subprocess.run( [simulator, "-q", "-c", "-", ihx], input=commands, text=True, capture_output=True, timeout=60, check=True, ) stop = re.search(r"^Stop at 0x([0-9a-fA-F]+):.*Breakpoint", result.stdout, re.MULTILINE) if not stop or int(stop.group(1), 16) != address: raise SystemExit(f"Simulator did not reach test_done:\n{result.stdout}\n{result.stderr}") match = re.search(r"^0x0*e000\s+([0-9a-fA-F]{2})\b", result.stdout, re.MULTILINE) if not match: raise SystemExit(f"Could not read simulator test result:\n{result.stdout}\n{result.stderr}") code = int(match.group(1), 16) if code: raise SystemExit(f"Z80 {name} test failed at checkpoint {code}:\n{result.stdout}") print(f"Z80 {name} test passed") def main(): simulator, ihx, map_file = sys.argv[1:] run_test(simulator, ihx, map_file, "RLE") if __name__ == "__main__": main()

diff --git a/home/stevepro/Steven/Code/testing2/TransbotCnew/README.md b/home/stevepro/Steven/Code/testing2/TransbotCnew/README.md index 0000000..0000000 100644 --- a/home/stevepro/Steven/Code/testing2/TransbotCnew/README.md +++ b/home/stevepro/Steven/Code/testing2/TransbotCnew/README.md @@ -1,67 +1,67 @@ # Transbot hybrid C / Z80 port This is a new, independent vertical slice; `../TransbotC` and `../TransbotASM` are not modified. The `dev/engine` and `dev/screen` layout follows the style of `../VanHalenDemoSMS`. The goal is to preserve original behavior with C-callable assembly first, then replace routines with C/SMSlib selectively. ## Build and test Requires SDCC 4.2.0 with `ihx2sms` and `sz80`, Python 3 and make, plus the included `../sverx420/devkitSMS` libraries. Run: ```sh cd TransbotCnew make test make ``` Open `build/transbot.sms` in Emulicious or another SMS emulator. `make clean` removes generated build files. The build extracts title assets from `../TransbotASM/TransBot.sms` after checking its MD5. Do not distribute the generated ROM or assets unless you have the necessary rights. `make test` executes the actual SDCC-assembled decoder in the `sz80` Z80 simulator and checks independently recorded hashes for all six asset streams. The exporter also validates stream boundaries and decoded buffer sizes before the unbounded original-style assembly is allowed to consume them. It also runs the assembled recorded-demo-input routine against boundary cases and the full 1,516-frame original input stream. ## Slice and correspondence | Original | This project | | --- | --- | | `engine/vdp_manager.inc`, `$291A` | `dev/engine/rle_manager.c`: Z80 instructions adapted to SDCC syntax, with C-callable 2- and 4-lane entry points. The wrapper preserves IX and uses a relocatable buffer instead of fixed `$D000`. | | `engine/boot_manager.inc`, `$2902`, `$28F0`, `$28F6`, `$28FC` | `dev/engine/content_manager.c`: original boot graphics decoded by the assembly routine and uploaded with SMSlib. | | `screen/title_screen.inc`, `engine/text_manager.inc` `$255E` | `dev/screen/title_screen.c` and content manager: palette, title graphic and fixed text. | | `engine/screen_manager.inc` `$0429` (scroll-related half) | `dev/screen/game_screen.c`: palette, stage sprite tiles (`$5116`), stage map decoded to the fixed buffer at `$D000` (`$4446`), cleared name table/SAT, original RAM block cleared. Press button 1/2 on the title to enter: the stage-1 background scrolls. No objects, HUD or input yet. | | `engine/scroll_manager.inc` `$2957-$2D00`, `$2D01-$2D6F`, VDP helpers `$27F7`, `$285B`, VBlank column streaming from `engine/interrupt_manager.inc` | `dev/engine/scroll_manager_z80.s`: **generated** from the original by `tools/port_asm.py` (WLA to sdasz80 syntax). `make test` assembles it at the original addresses and requires every byte to equal the ROM, and that the checked-in file is up to date. `dev/engine/scroll_manager.c` wraps it; `game_screen.c` ports the `$0FA6` VBlank/line scroll-register writes to SMSlib interrupt hooks. | | `engine/input_manager.inc` `$2519`, `screen/game_screen.inc` `$0347` | `dev/engine/demo_input_manager.c`: original recorded-input logic, with relocatable state and stream. Tested independently, not yet connected to the ROM's main loop. | | Reset/IRQ/NMI vectors | devkitSMS CRT and SMSlib for now, not the original vector handlers. | | Original sound engine | Not ported. PSGlib is linked and silenced; no song is played. Original song bytes are not PSGlib format. | The title map's second RLE lane continues into the following original ROM data before its terminator. The extractor intentionally includes those bytes, validates both lanes, and the assembly reproduces the complete decode. Only the first 68 words are copied to the screen, as in the original. This is **not playable**: title timing, score digits, controller transitions, gameplay objects/HUD/collision and the stage sequencer, original sound, reset/pause semantics, and raster IRQ behavior remain to be ported. The input playback code is not linked into the title-only ROM yet: the demo requires the original gameplay object, map, collision and input routines as well. It holds the title indefinitely. Library startup and interrupt timing differ from the original, so the resulting ROM is not expected to match its MD5. Compare display, timing, input and sound against the original in an emulator as later slices are added. ## RAM layout The ported original routines use fixed RAM (`$C000-$C61F` variables, `$D000` decode buffer). The link uses `--data-loc 0xC620` so SDCC and SMSlib variables sit above the original block, and `engine_rle_buffer` is fixed at `$D000` (`dev/engine/original_ram.h` names the original variables used from C). In the original, `ld hl, _DATA_8_`/`ld de, _DATA_F_` use ROM addresses as the literals 8 and 15; the converter emits those as plain numbers.

diff --git a/home/stevepro/Steven/Code/testing2/TransbotCnew/tools/export_assets.py b/home/stevepro/Steven/Code/testing2/TransbotCnew/tools/export_assets.py index 0000000..0000000 100644 --- a/home/stevepro/Steven/Code/testing2/TransbotCnew/tools/export_assets.py +++ b/home/stevepro/Steven/Code/testing2/TransbotCnew/tools/export_assets.py @@ -1,200 +1,200 @@ #!/usr/bin/env python3 """Extract the recorded demo input from the verified original ROM. The graphics and code come straight from the converted original assembly, so the demo stream (which has no label in the disassembly) is the only exported asset.""" import hashlib import os from pathlib import Path import sys import tempfile EXPECTED_MD5 = "dd26c323d1323691fed04cd8498afbcf" def validate_demo(data): if len(data) != 539 or data[-1] != 0: raise ValueError("incorrect demo stream length or terminator") for pos in range(0, len(data) - 1, 2): if not data[pos] or data[pos + 1] & 0xC0: raise ValueError(f"invalid demo input pair at offset {pos}") def main(): if len(sys.argv) != 3: raise SystemExit("usage: export_assets.py ROM OUTPUT") rom = Path(sys.argv[1]).read_bytes() actual = hashlib.md5(rom).hexdigest() if actual != EXPECTED_MD5: raise SystemExit(f"Unexpected ROM checksum: {actual} (expected {EXPECTED_MD5})") demo = rom[0x7A80:0x7C9B] validate_demo(demo) assets = [("demo_input", demo)] lines = ["/* Generated from the verified original ROM. Do not edit. */", ""] for name, data in assets: lines.append(f"static const unsigned char {name}[] = {{") for offset in range(0, len(data), 16): lines.append(" " + ", ".join(f"0x{value:02x}" for value in data[offset:offset + 16]) + ",") lines.extend(("};", "")) output = Path(sys.argv[2]) temporary = None try: with tempfile.NamedTemporaryFile(mode="w", dir=output.parent, delete=False) as file: temporary = file.name file.write("\n".join(lines)) os.replace(temporary, output) finally: if temporary and os.path.exists(temporary): os.unlink(temporary) if __name__ == "__main__": main()

cd /home/stevepro/Steven/Code/testing2/TransbotCnew; cat >> README.md <<'E'

## Gameplay (object system) slice

`tools/port_asm.py convert` turns the original gameplay files (objects, stage,
map, scroll, score, collision, graphics data) into `build/original_game.s`,
which is linked with the C code; `dev/engine/original_game.h` declares it. The
graphics data lives in ROM bank 2, which `main.c` maps once. `game_screen.c`
runs the original per-frame routines from the SMSlib frame interrupt and the
main loop.

Findings worth keeping:

- The original init table (`$003B`) sets VDP register 6 to `$FB`, so sprite
  tiles come from VRAM `$0000`. SMSlib defaults to `$2000` (the boot font), so
  `screen_game_screen_load()` writes `$FB` to register 6.
- The original object code writes to the VDP from the main loop, which the
  original ran with its own interrupt timing. Under SMSlib an interrupt between
  the two bytes of a VDP command corrupted registers (the player sprite turned
  into font letters), so `screen_game_screen_update()` runs with interrupts
  masked.
- ROM pointers that the original compared as literals (`$2B49`, `$2C0B`) are
  rewritten to symbols by the converter, since the code is relocated.
- `make test` also checks the stage streams inside the built ROM against the
  original and runs the original decoders and scroll setup in `sz80`.
E
echo ok
Terminal output
ok
Yes, the red letters were the player sprite. Its graphics were wrong because of two bugs, and I've fixed both. The ship now draws correctly and stays correct, and the stage, HUD, "PLAYER-1 START" banner and parallax look right.

Headless emulator check. Genesis Plus GX does emulate the Master System, and I used it through ~/.copilot/session-state/…/files/sms_probe.c to run the ROM without opening a window. It reads the game's RAM and writes screenshots at chosen frames.

What was wrong:
1. Wrong sprite tile base. The original game sets VDP register 6 to $FB, so sprite tiles come from VRAM $0000. SMSlib defaults to $2000, where the boot font is stored. I now write $FB in screen_game_screen_load() in game_screen.c.
2. An interrupt race. After that fix the ship was right at first, then turned into letters. The six tile numbers 0c,05,00,06,07,0a spell L,E,space,F,G,J in the font's order, so register 6 had been reset to $FF. The original object code writes to the VDP from the main loop. If SMSlib's interrupt landed between the two bytes of a VDP command, the registers were corrupted. Masking interrupts around screen_game_screen_update() fixed it, and the ship is still correct at frame 898.

I also fixed two relocated pointers ($2B49 and $2C0B) in the converter, and set up the original's interrupt-disabled setup sequence.

Tests.
- make test all passes.
- New sz80 checks cover the original stage decoders, the scroll setup, and the screen setup and stage start.
- A new ROM test compares the stage data inside build/transbot.sms with the original.
- The README is updated with these findings.

Caveats and open items:
- Interrupts are now off for the whole update. If it ever runs longer than a frame, the line-interrupt scroll split could jitter. I haven't measured the update time. The cleaner fix is to mask interrupts only around the VDP writes.
- No enemies appeared in the first 15 seconds with no input. I haven't checked whether the original spawns them that early.
- Player movement and shooting haven't been tested yet. The probe can inject button presses for that.

The probe is a scratch tool kept in the session folder, not in the project.

"""Relocated code must not keep ROM-address literals that point at inline data."""

from pathlib import Path
import re
import sys
import unittest

ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]
sys.path.insert(0, str(ROOT / "tools"))
from port_asm import DATA_POINTERS  # noqa: E402


class RelocationTests(unittest.TestCase):
    def test_data_pointers_are_symbolic(self):
        source = (ROOT / "build/original_game.s").read_text()
        for address in DATA_POINTERS:
            with self.subTest(address=hex(address)):
                self.assertIsNone(re.search(rf"#0x{address:04X}\b", source, re.IGNORECASE))


if __name__ == "__main__":
    unittest.main()

Terminal output
-rw-rw-r-- 1 stevepro stevepro 32768 Nov 12 19:24 build/transbot.sms
The ROM is now 32 KB like the original. The 48 KB came from my putting the graphics in a separate bank 2, and I've removed that along with the bank switching.

Problems found and fixed on the way:
1. Overlap with the SEGA header. At 32 KB, the startup code landed on top of the header at $7FF0, which would have broken the cartridge. The tests didn't catch it, so I added a check that the ROM is 32,768 bytes and that the header still reads TMR SEGA. To make room I dropped PSGlib and replaced it with the same four PSG-mute writes the original boot code uses. Audio was already deferred. The original's sound engine isn't PSGlib format, so I plan to port it as Z80 code.
2. A hang at around 10 seconds of play, which is probably why the enemy graphics looked wrong. Enemy code loads its animation tables by their original ROM addresses, for example ld hl,$1FD6. Once the code was relocated, those addresses pointed at unrelated bytes. In the first 48 KB build this happened to work by luck. After the change the game hung with RAM full of 0x3f. The converter in port_asm.py now turns the 15 table addresses (DATA_POINTERS) into label references, including ones into the middle of a table. A new test, tests/test_relocation.py, fails if a numeric address comes back.

Results.
- make test all passes, including the byte-for-byte check of the converted code against the original ROM.
- The ROM is 31,068 bytes used of 32,768.
- A 30-second headless run (1,800 frames, no input) has no hang. Red spiked mines now fly in at about 14 seconds, and the player ship, HUD and parallax all look right.
- I did not run it in Emulicious or on real hardware.

Next steps. The next steps are to test player movement and firing with injected button presses, and to check the interrupt masking around the main-loop update on frame timing. Tell me which you'd like to do first.

rom=open('TransbotASM/TransBot.sms','rb').read()
def dec(addr,lanes):
    buf={}; pos=addr
    for lane in range(lanes):
        o=lane
        while True:
            a=rom[pos]
            if a==0: pos+=1; break
            if a&0x80:
                n=a&0x7f
                for i in range(n): pos+=1; buf[o]=rom[pos]; o+=lanes
                pos+=1
            else:
                pos+=1; v=rom[pos]
                for i in range(a): buf[o]=v; o+=lanes
                pos+=1
    return bytes(buf.get(i,0) for i in range(max(buf)+1))
tiles=dec(0x5116,4); print('decoded stage tiles',len(tiles))
needle=tiles[:64]
res={}
for name in ('new','org'):
    st=open(f'/tmp/st_{name}.bin','rb').read()
    i=st.find(needle); print(name,'found at',hex(i) if i>=0 else None)
    res[name]=(st,i)
base_new=res['new'][1]-0; base_org=res['org'][1]
# VRAM base = found - 0x2C00
vn=base_new-0x2C00; vo=base_org-0x2C00
print('vram base',hex(vn),hex(vo))
for name,v in (('new',vn),('org',vo)):
    st=res[name][0]
    nt=st[v+0x3800:v+0x3800+0x700]
    print(name,'rows 15..23 name table (tile numbers, hex3), cols 16-31')
    for r in range(15,24):
        row=[nt[r*64+c*2]|((nt[r*64+c*2+1]&1)<<8) for c in range(32)]
        print(f'{r:2d}',' '.join('%03x'%x for x in row[16:]))

def sub(p,a,b):
    s=open(p).read(); assert a in s,(p,a); open(p,'w').write(s.replace(a,b,1))
sub('dev/crt0_sms.s',''';--------------------------------------------------------------------------
; ***** this is a modified version aimed to SMS homebrew - sverx\\2022 *****
;--------------------------------------------------------------------------''',''';--------------------------------------------------------------------------
; ***** this is a modified version aimed to SMS homebrew - sverx\\2022 *****
; ***** TransbotCnew: copy of devkitSMS crt0_sms.s with one change: the *****
; ***** startup clears all work RAM ($C000-$DFEE), not just l__DATA bytes. *****
; ***** The original game's variables live at $C000-$C61F and SDCC data *****
; ***** starts at $C620, so the stock clear never reached SMSlib's own  *****
; ***** variables (handler pointers, key state) on emulators/hardware   *****
; ***** that do not power on with zeroed RAM.                           *****
;--------------------------------------------------------------------------''')
sub('dev/crt0_sms.s','        ld bc,#l__DATA-1                ;   for the unitialized statics','        ld bc,#0x1eee                   ;   $C001-$DFEE (the stack at $DFF0 is not touched)')
sub('Makefile','CRT0 = $(DEVKIT)/crt0/crt0_sms.rel','CRT0 = $(BUILD)/crt0_sms.rel')
sub('Makefile','$(BUILD)/original_game.s:','$(BUILD)/crt0_sms.rel: dev/crt0_sms.s | $(BUILD)\n\t$(AS) -plosgff $@ $<\n\n$(BUILD)/original_game.s:')
sub('Makefile','$(BUILD)/rle_manager.* $(BUILD)/title_screen.* $(BUILD)/game_screen.* $(BUILD)/original_game.* \\','$(BUILD)/rle_manager.* $(BUILD)/title_screen.* $(BUILD)/game_screen.* $(BUILD)/original_game.* \\\n\t\t$(BUILD)/crt0_sms.* \\')

python3 - <<'E'
rom=open('TransbotASM/TransBot.sms','rb').read()
def dec(addr,lanes):
    buf={}; pos=addr
    for lane in range(lanes):
        o=lane
        while True:
            a=rom[pos]
            if a==0: pos+=1; break
            if a&0x80:
                for i in range(a&0x7f): pos+=1; buf[o]=rom[pos]; o+=lanes
                pos+=1
            else:
                pos+=1; v=rom[pos]
                for i in range(a): buf[o]=v; o+=lanes
                pos+=1
    return bytes(buf.get(i,0) for i in range(max(buf)+1))
tiles=dec(0x5116,4)
needle=tiles[0x100:0x140]
S={}
for n in ('new','org'):
    st=open(f'/tmp/st_{n}.bin','rb').read(); i=st.find(needle)
    print(n,'tile data at',hex(i)); S[n]=(st,i-0x2C00-0x100)
import pickle; pickle.dump(S,open('/tmp/S.pkl','wb'))
for n,(st,v) in S.items():
    nt=st[v+0x3800:v+0x3800+0x700]
    print(n,'name table rows 15..23, cols 16..31 (tile idx)')
    for r in range(15,24):
        print(f'{r:2d}',' '.join('%03x'%(nt[r*64+c*2]|((nt[r*64+c*2+1]&1)<<8)) for c in range(16,32)))

    s=open(p).read(); assert a in s,(p,a); open(p,'w').write(s.replace(a,b,1))

__sfr __at (0xbe) vdp_data_port;

/*
 * Copies to VRAM without SMSlib's helpers: those re-enable interrupts, and the
 * original handler keeps them off, so a line interrupt cannot corrupt the VDP
 * address in the middle of a transfer.
 */
static void vram_write(unsigned int address, const unsigned char *source, unsigned int size)
{
    SMS_VDPControlPort = (unsigned char)address;
    SMS_VDPControlPort = (unsigned char)(address >> 8) | 0x40;
    do {
        vdp_data_port = *source++;
    } while (--size);
}''')

  middle of the display-off screen draw and drop name-table cells (black tiles) and,
  later, corrupt VDP registers (the player sprite turned into font letters). The
  frame handler in `game_screen.c` therefore uses plain port writes only.

  RAM (`$C000-$DFEE`). The stock file clears only `l__DATA` bytes from `$C000`, which
  missed SMSlib's variables (handler pointers) once `--data-loc` moved to `$C620`.
  That worked on emulators that zero RAM but halted on Emulicious and would on
  hardware. Check with a random-RAM start when changing startup code.

sed -n '/Findings worth keeping/,$p' README.md | head -40; export PATH=$PATH:/home/stevepro/sdcc/bin; make test all 2>&1 | grep -E 'Info: [0-9]|passed|OK$|FAIL|rror|Traceback'; ls -l build/transbot.sms
Terminal output
Findings worth keeping: - The original init table (`$003B`) sets VDP register 6 to `$FB`, so sprite tiles come from VRAM `$0000`. SMSlib defaults to `$2000` (the boot font), so `screen_game_screen_load()` writes `$FB` to register 6. - The original VBlank handler keeps interrupts disabled until its final `ei`. SMSlib helpers such as `SMS_VRAMmemcpy` re-enable them, which let a line interrupt hit the middle of the display-off screen draw and drop name-table cells (black tiles) and, later, corrupt VDP registers (the player sprite turned into font letters). The frame handler in `game_screen.c` therefore uses plain port writes only. - `dev/crt0_sms.s` is the devkitSMS startup file with one change: it clears all work RAM (`$C000-$DFEE`). The stock file clears only `l__DATA` bytes from `$C000`, which missed SMSlib's variables (handler pointers) once `--data-loc` moved to `$C620`. That worked on emulators that zero RAM but halted on Emulicious and would on hardware. Check with a random-RAM start when changing startup code. - ROM addresses used as literals (`$2B49`, `$2C0B`, and the inline enemy animation tables such as `$1FD6`, listed in `DATA_POINTERS`) are rewritten to symbols by the converter, since the code is relocated. Leaving them as numbers made enemies read random ROM bytes and hung the game. - `ihx2sms` warns when the ROM exceeds 32 KB; check the `_GSINIT` area in `build/transbot.map` ends below `$7FF0`, which `tests/test_rom_assets.py` also checks. - `make test` also checks the stage streams inside the built ROM against the original and runs the original decoders and scroll setup in `sz80`. OK Z80 RLE test passed Z80 demo input test passed -rw-rw-r-- 1 stevepro stevepro 32768 Nov 12 19:47 build/transbot.sms
I've fixed both problems and rebuilt build/transbot.sms. I couldn't run Emulicious or Kega Fusion here, so please re-test on both. My checks used the Genesis Plus GX core with random power-on RAM, and make test all passes.

Black screen on Emulicious
- Cause: the devkit startup code only clears about 254 bytes of RAM from $C000. I had moved the SDCC variables up to $C620 to leave room for the original game's RAM, so SMSlib's own variables were never cleared. That includes the interrupt handler pointers. Emulators that zero RAM hid this. Emulicious and real hardware don't, so the first interrupt jumped to a garbage address.
- Fix: I added dev/crt0_sms.s, a copy of the devkit startup file whose only change is clearing $C000–$DFEE. The Makefile builds and links it.
- Test: I filled RAM with random bytes before power-on and ran six seeds. All six boot to identical frames.

Black tiles in the background (Kega Fusion)
- Cause: the sprite upload used SMSlib's SMS_VRAMmemcpy, which turns interrupts back on inside my frame handler. The original handler keeps them off. A line interrupt could then fire in the middle of the first screen draw and corrupt the VDP address. That dropped name-table cells.
- Evidence: VRAM dumps showed five cells still holding the clear value (row 17 columns 21–23, row 23 columns 30–31) in my ROM but not in the original.
- Fix: the handler now uses plain port writes for the sprite table. The black cells are gone at the first frames and at later frames.
- Related change: this was probably also what turned the player sprite into letters earlier. With it fixed, I removed the di/ei I had added around the main-loop update. Frame output is identical with and without it.

What the test run shows
- The ROM is still 32 KB (31,092 bytes used) and the SEGA header is intact.
- In a 30-second headless run with no input, the player is hit by enemies. The score reaches 400, a life is lost, and "PLAYER-1 START" appears again. Gameplay is running.
- The README now documents the startup RAM clear and the interrupt finding.

If either emulator still misbehaves, tell me which one and what you see. Emulicious would be the more useful report, because it's the one I can't check here.

Yes, exactly. I copied [crt0_sms.s](/home/stevepro/Steven/Code/testing2/TransbotCnew/dev/crt0_sms.s) from your existing [devkitSMS source](/home/stevepro/Steven/Code/testing2/sverx420/devkitSMS/crt0/src/crt0_sms.s). The copyright and licence comments were already there and have been preserved.

CRT0 is the startup code that runs before C’s main(): it sets up the stack, initializes RAM and SMSlib, and connects the interrupt handlers.

I made one code change in the project copy: it clears RAM through $DFEE, rather than clearing only the amount specified by l__DATA. This ensures SMSlib’s variables at $C620 are initialized. The original devkit file remains untouched.
Conversation 24 · 12 Nov 2026 19:54

You

Interesting - I have written many games using devkitSMS and crt0_sms.rel but I've never used crt0_sms.s
CAn you pls repeat why this was added to transbot? I think it was to clear our more RAM?

GitHub Copilot

Yes—it was added to clear more RAM, because Transbot uses an unusual RAM layout.

Normally, devkitSMS places its C variables starting at $C000. Its existing crt0_sms.rel is the assembled startup code, so you don't need the .s source in a typical project.

For Transbot, we reserved $C000–$C61F for the original game's fixed-address variables and moved SDCC/SMSlib variables to $C620. The stock startup code still begins clearing at $C000 and clears only l__DATA bytes. In this build, that didn't reach the relocated SMSlib variables—including interrupt-handler pointers.

I therefore copied the startup source into [crt0_sms.s](/home/stevepro/Steven/Code/testing2/TransbotCnew/dev/crt0_sms.s), changed the clear length, and had the Makefile assemble it into the .rel file used by Transbot. Your devkitSMS copy is unchanged.

One correction: the comment in the project copy says it clears through $DFEE, but the actual instruction clears through $DEEE. That is still enough to clear the relocated variables and the original game's work RAM. My earlier explanation repeated the incorrect comment; sorry about that. Also, the random-RAM test supported this as the cause of the Emulicious startup issue, but I haven't independently confirmed it in Emulicious.

SMSlib's handler preserves AF/BC/DE/HL/IX/IY, but the * original code also uses the alternate register set, so this wrapper saves it. */ static void game_frame_interrupt(void) __naked { __asm .db 0x08 ; exchange af with the alternate af push af .db 0x08 ; exchange af with the alternate af exx push bc push de push hl exx call _game_frame_body exx pop hl pop de pop bc exx .db 0x08 ; exchange af with the alternate af pop af .db 0x08 ; exchange af with the alternate af ret __endasm; } static void game_frame_body(void) { INLINE_SMS_setBGScrollX(ORIG_SCROLL_X_TOP); SMS_VDPControlPort = 0x7f; SMS_VDPControlPort = 0x8a; if (ORIG_FRAME_COUNTER < 0x0f) ++ORIG_FRAME_COUNTER; if (ORIG_FRAME_READY) return; if (ORIG_SAT_FLAGS & 0x80) { vram_write(0x3f00, (const unsigned char *)0xc240, 0x40); vram_write(0x3f80, (const unsigned char *)0xc280, 0x80); ORIG_SAT_FLAGS &= 0x7f; } orig_vblank_columns(); orig_scroll_advance(); orig_input_update(); orig_player_update(); orig_timer_update(); orig_score_update(); orig_hud_update(); orig_stage_timer(); ORIG_FRAME_READY = 1; } /* Line-interrupt part of $0FA6: scroll for the lower region of the screen. */ static void game_line_interrupt(void) { if (ORIG_SCROLL_FLAGS & SCROLL_FLAG_BONUS) return; INLINE_SMS_setBGScrollX(ORIG_SCROLL_X_BOTTOM); SMS_VDPControlPort = 0xff; SMS_VDPControlPort = 0x8a; } /* Gameplay start of the original state 4 ($03B2): stage 1, one player. */ void screen_game_screen_load(void) { SMS_displayOff(); SMS_setFrameInterruptHandler(0); SMS_setLineInterruptHandler(0); memset((void *)ORIGINAL_RAM_START, 0, ORIGINAL_RAM_SIZE); memset((void *)ORIGINAL_WORK_RAM_START, 0, ORIGINAL_WORK_RAM_SIZE); ORIG_FRAME_READY = 0; ORIG_FRAME_COUNTER = 0; ORIG_PAD_DETECT = 0xff; ORIG_MODE_FLAGS = 0x50; /* The original performs screen and object initialization with interrupts disabled. */ __asm di __endasm; orig_screen_setup(); orig_stage_start(); ORIG_STAGE_STATE = 1; /* The original init table ($003B) puts sprite tiles at VRAM $0000; SMSlib defaults to $2000. */ SMS_VDPControlPort = 0xfb; SMS_VDPControlPort = 0x86; __asm ei __endasm; SMS_setLineCounter(0x7f); SMS_setFrameInterruptHandler(game_frame_interrupt); SMS_setLineInterruptHandler(game_line_interrupt); SMS_enableLineInterrupt(); SMS_displayOn(); } /* Main-loop part of the original game state: runs once per completed VBlank. */ void screen_game_screen_update(void) { if (!ORIG_FRAME_READY) return; diff --git a/home/stevepro/Steven/Code/testing2/TransbotCnew/README.md b/home/stevepro/Steven/Code/testing2/TransbotCnew/README.md index 0000000..0000000 100644 --- a/home/stevepro/Steven/Code/testing2/TransbotCnew/README.md +++ b/home/stevepro/Steven/Code/testing2/TransbotCnew/README.md @@ -1,101 +1,101 @@ # Transbot hybrid C / Z80 port This is a new, independent vertical slice; `../TransbotC` and `../TransbotASM` are not modified. The `dev/engine` and `dev/screen` layout follows the style of `../VanHalenDemoSMS`. The goal is to preserve original behavior with C-callable assembly first, then replace routines with C/SMSlib selectively. ## Build and test Requires SDCC 4.2.0 with `ihx2sms` and `sz80`, Python 3 and make, plus the included `../sverx420/devkitSMS` libraries. Run: ```sh cd TransbotCnew make test make ``` Open `build/transbot.sms` in Emulicious or another SMS emulator. `make clean` removes generated build files. The build extracts title assets from `../TransbotASM/TransBot.sms` after checking its MD5. Do not distribute the generated ROM or assets unless you have the necessary rights. `make test` executes the actual SDCC-assembled decoder in the `sz80` Z80 simulator and checks independently recorded hashes for all six asset streams. The exporter also validates stream boundaries and decoded buffer sizes before the unbounded original-style assembly is allowed to consume them. It also runs the assembled recorded-demo-input routine against boundary cases and the full 1,516-frame original input stream. ## Slice and correspondence | Original | This project | | --- | --- | | `engine/vdp_manager.inc`, `$291A` | `dev/engine/rle_manager.c`: Z80 instructions adapted to SDCC syntax, with C-callable 2- and 4-lane entry points. The wrapper preserves IX and uses a relocatable buffer instead of fixed `$D000`. | | `engine/boot_manager.inc`, `$2902`, `$28F0`, `$28F6`, `$28FC` | `dev/engine/content_manager.c`: original boot graphics decoded by the assembly routine and uploaded with SMSlib. | | `screen/title_screen.inc`, `engine/text_manager.inc` `$255E` | `dev/screen/title_screen.c` and content manager: palette, title graphic and fixed text. | | `engine/screen_manager.inc` `$0429` (scroll-related half) | `dev/screen/game_screen.c`: palette, stage sprite tiles (`$5116`), stage map decoded to the fixed buffer at `$D000` (`$4446`), cleared name table/SAT, original RAM block cleared. Press button 1/2 on the title to enter: the stage-1 background scrolls. No objects, HUD or input yet. | | `engine/scroll_manager.inc` `$2957-$2D00`, `$2D01-$2D6F`, VDP helpers `$27F7`, `$285B`, VBlank column streaming from `engine/interrupt_manager.inc` | `dev/engine/scroll_manager_z80.s`: **generated** from the original by `tools/port_asm.py` (WLA to sdasz80 syntax). `make test` assembles it at the original addresses and requires every byte to equal the ROM, and that the checked-in file is up to date. `dev/engine/scroll_manager.c` wraps it; `game_screen.c` ports the `$0FA6` VBlank/line scroll-register writes to SMSlib interrupt hooks. | | `engine/input_manager.inc` `$2519`, `screen/game_screen.inc` `$0347` | `dev/engine/demo_input_manager.c`: original recorded-input logic, with relocatable state and stream. Tested independently, not yet connected to the ROM's main loop. | | Reset/IRQ/NMI vectors | devkitSMS CRT and SMSlib for now, not the original vector handlers. | | Original sound engine | Not ported. PSGlib is not linked (the ROM must fit in 32 KB like the original); the PSG is muted at start. Original song bytes are not PSGlib format, so the original sound engine will be ported as Z80 code. | The title map's second RLE lane continues into the following original ROM data before its terminator. The extractor intentionally includes those bytes, validates both lanes, and the assembly reproduces the complete decode. Only the first 68 words are copied to the screen, as in the original. This is **not playable**: title timing, score digits, controller transitions, gameplay objects/HUD/collision and the stage sequencer, original sound, reset/pause semantics, and raster IRQ behavior remain to be ported. The input playback code is not linked into the title-only ROM yet: the demo requires the original gameplay object, map, collision and input routines as well. It holds the title indefinitely. Library startup and interrupt timing differ from the original, so the resulting ROM is not expected to match its MD5. Compare display, timing, input and sound against the original in an emulator as later slices are added. ## RAM layout The ported original routines use fixed RAM (`$C000-$C61F` variables, `$D000` decode buffer). The link uses `--data-loc 0xC620` so SDCC and SMSlib variables sit above the original block, and `engine_rle_buffer` is fixed at `$D000` (`dev/engine/original_ram.h` names the original variables used from C). In the original, `ld hl, _DATA_8_`/`ld de, _DATA_F_` use ROM addresses as the literals 8 and 15; the converter emits those as plain numbers. ## Gameplay (object system) slice `tools/port_asm.py convert` turns the original gameplay files (objects, stage, map, scroll, score, collision, graphics data) into `build/original_game.s`, which is linked with the C code; `dev/engine/original_game.h` declares it. The graphics data is linked right after the code, so the ROM is 32 KB like the original (no bank switching). `game_screen.c` runs the original per-frame routines from the SMSlib frame interrupt and the main loop. Findings worth keeping: - The original init table (`$003B`) sets VDP register 6 to `$FB`, so sprite tiles come from VRAM `$0000`. SMSlib defaults to `$2000` (the boot font), so `screen_game_screen_load()` writes `$FB` to register 6. - The original VBlank handler keeps interrupts disabled until its final `ei`. SMSlib helpers such as `SMS_VRAMmemcpy` re-enable them, which let a line interrupt hit the middle of the display-off screen draw and drop name-table cells (black tiles) and, later, corrupt VDP registers (the player sprite turned into font letters). The frame handler in `game_screen.c` therefore uses plain port writes only. - `dev/crt0_sms.s` is the devkitSMS startup file with one change: it clears all work RAM (`$C000-$DFEE`). The stock file clears only `l__DATA` bytes from `$C000`, which missed SMSlib's variables (handler pointers) once `--data-loc` moved to `$C620`. That worked on emulators that zero RAM but halted on Emulicious and would on hardware. Check with a random-RAM start when changing startup code. - ROM addresses used as literals (`$2B49`, `$2C0B`, and the inline enemy animation tables such as `$1FD6`, listed in `DATA_POINTERS`) are rewritten to symbols by the converter, since the code is relocated. Leaving them as numbers made enemies read random ROM bytes and hung the game. - `ihx2sms` warns when the ROM exceeds 32 KB; check the `_GSINIT` area in `build/transbot.map` ends below `$7FF0`, which `tests/test_rom_assets.py` also checks. - `make test` also checks the stage streams inside the built ROM against the original and runs the original decoders and scroll setup in `sz80`.

diff --git a/home/stevepro/Steven/Code/testing2/TransbotCnew/tests/run_rle_test.py b/home/stevepro/Steven/Code/testing2/TransbotCnew/tests/run_rle_test.py index 0000000..0000000 100644 --- a/home/stevepro/Steven/Code/testing2/TransbotCnew/tests/run_rle_test.py +++ b/home/stevepro/Steven/Code/testing2/TransbotCnew/tests/run_rle_test.py @@ -1,40 +1,40 @@ #!/usr/bin/env python3 """Run the compiled Z80 decoder tests in the SDCC simulator.""" from pathlib import Path import re import subprocess import sys def run_test(simulator, ihx, map_file, name): map_text = Path(map_file).read_text() match = re.search(r"^\s*([0-9A-Fa-f]+)\s+_test_done\s", map_text, re.MULTILINE) if not match: raise SystemExit("Cannot find test_done in linker map") address = int(match.group(1), 16) commands = f"run 0 0x{address:x}\ndump /x rom 0xe000 0xb000\nquit\n" result = subprocess.run( [simulator, "-q", "-c", "-", ihx], input=commands, text=True, capture_output=True, timeout=60, check=True, ) stop = re.search(r"^Stop at 0x([0-9a-fA-F]+):.*Breakpoint", result.stdout, re.MULTILINE) if not stop or int(stop.group(1), 16) != address: raise SystemExit(f"Simulator did not reach test_done:\n{result.stdout}\n{result.stderr}") match = re.search(r"^0x0*e000\s+([0-9a-fA-F]{2})\b", result.stdout, re.MULTILINE) if not match: raise SystemExit(f"Could not read simulator test result:\n{result.stdout}\n{result.stderr}") code = int(match.group(1), 16) if code: raise SystemExit(f"Z80 {name} test failed at checkpoint {code}:\n{result.stdout}") print(f"Z80 {name} test passed") def main(): simulator, ihx, map_file = sys.argv[1:] run_test(simulator, ihx, map_file, "RLE") if __name__ == "__main__": main()
