diff --git a/home/stevepro/Steven/Code/testing2/TransbotCnew/README.md b/home/stevepro/Steven/Code/testing2/TransbotCnew/README.md index 0000000..0000000 100644 --- a/home/stevepro/Steven/Code/testing2/TransbotCnew/README.md +++ b/home/stevepro/Steven/Code/testing2/TransbotCnew/README.md @@ -1,106 +1,106 @@ # Transbot hybrid C / Z80 port This is a new, independent vertical slice; `../TransbotC` and `../TransbotASM` are not modified. The `dev/engine` and `dev/screen` layout follows the style of `../VanHalenDemoSMS`. The goal is to preserve original behavior with C-callable assembly first, then replace routines with C/SMSlib selectively. ## Build and test Requires SDCC 4.2.0 with `ihx2sms` and `sz80`, Python 3 and make, plus the included `../sverx420/devkitSMS` libraries. Run: ```sh cd TransbotCnew make test make ``` Open `build/transbot.sms` in Emulicious or another SMS emulator. `make clean` removes generated build files. The build extracts title assets from `../TransbotASM/TransBot.sms` after checking its MD5. Do not distribute the generated ROM or assets unless you have the necessary rights. `make test` executes the actual SDCC-assembled decoder in the `sz80` Z80 simulator and checks independently recorded hashes for all six asset streams. The exporter also validates stream boundaries and decoded buffer sizes before the unbounded original-style assembly is allowed to consume them. It also runs the assembled recorded-demo-input routine against boundary cases and the full 1,516-frame original input stream. ## Slice and correspondence | Original | This project | | --- | --- | | `engine/vdp_manager.inc`, `$291A` | `dev/engine/rle_manager.c`: Z80 instructions adapted to SDCC syntax, with C-callable 2- and 4-lane entry points. The wrapper preserves IX and uses a relocatable buffer instead of fixed `$D000`. | | `engine/boot_manager.inc`, `$2902`, `$28F0`, `$28F6`, `$28FC` | `dev/engine/content_manager.c`: original boot graphics decoded by the assembly routine and uploaded with SMSlib. | | `screen/title_screen.inc`, `engine/text_manager.inc` `$255E` | `dev/screen/title_screen.c` and content manager: palette, title graphic and fixed text. | | `engine/screen_manager.inc` `$0429` (scroll-related half) | `dev/screen/game_screen.c`: palette, stage sprite tiles (`$5116`), stage map decoded to the fixed buffer at `$D000` (`$4446`), cleared name table/SAT, original RAM block cleared. Press button 1/2 on the title to enter: the stage-1 background scrolls. No objects, HUD or input yet. | | `engine/scroll_manager.inc` `$2957-$2D00`, `$2D01-$2D6F`, VDP helpers `$27F7`, `$285B`, VBlank column streaming from `engine/interrupt_manager.inc` | `dev/engine/scroll_manager_z80.s`: **generated** from the original by `tools/port_asm.py` (WLA to sdasz80 syntax). `make test` assembles it at the original addresses and requires every byte to equal the ROM, and that the checked-in file is up to date. `dev/engine/scroll_manager.c` wraps it; `game_screen.c` ports the `$0FA6` VBlank/line scroll-register writes to SMSlib interrupt hooks. | | `engine/input_manager.inc` `$2519`, `screen/game_screen.inc` `$0347` | `dev/engine/demo_input_manager.c`: original recorded-input logic, with relocatable state and stream. Tested independently, not yet connected to the ROM's main loop. | | Reset/IRQ/NMI vectors | devkitSMS CRT and SMSlib for now, not the original vector handlers. | | Original sound engine | Not ported. PSGlib is not linked (the ROM must fit in 32 KB like the original); the PSG is muted at start. Original song bytes are not PSGlib format, so the original sound engine will be ported as Z80 code. | The title map's second RLE lane continues into the following original ROM data before its terminator. The extractor intentionally includes those bytes, validates both lanes, and the assembly reproduces the complete decode. Only the first 68 words are copied to the screen, as in the original. This is **not playable**: title timing, score digits, controller transitions, gameplay objects/HUD/collision and the stage sequencer, original sound, reset/pause semantics, and raster IRQ behavior remain to be ported. The input playback code is not linked into the title-only ROM yet: the demo requires the original gameplay object, map, collision and input routines as well. It holds the title indefinitely. Library startup and interrupt timing differ from the original, so the resulting ROM is not expected to match its MD5. Compare display, timing, input and sound against the original in an emulator as later slices are added. ## RAM layout The ported original routines use fixed RAM (`$C000-$C61F` variables, `$D000` decode buffer). The link uses `--data-loc 0xC620` so SDCC and SMSlib variables sit above the original block, and `engine_rle_buffer` is fixed at `$D000` (`dev/engine/original_ram.h` names the original variables used from C). In the original, `ld hl, _DATA_8_`/`ld de, _DATA_F_` use ROM addresses as the literals 8 and 15; the converter emits those as plain numbers. ## Gameplay (object system) slice `tools/port_asm.py convert` turns the original gameplay files (objects, stage, map, scroll, score, collision, graphics data) into `build/original_game.s`, which is linked with the C code; `dev/engine/original_game.h` declares it. The graphics data is linked right after the code, so the ROM is 32 KB like the original (no bank switching). `game_screen.c` runs the original per-frame routines from the SMSlib frame interrupt and the main loop. Gameplay initialization sets the original secret-command flags at `$C100` to `$0F` after clearing RAM and before screen setup. This enables all four flags, equivalent to replacing the original load from `$C012` with `ld a, $0f; nop`. The secret-select screen itself is not included. Findings worth keeping: - The original init table (`$003B`) sets VDP register 6 to `$FB`, so sprite tiles come from VRAM `$0000`. SMSlib defaults to `$2000` (the boot font), so `screen_game_screen_load()` writes `$FB` to register 6. - The original VBlank handler keeps interrupts disabled until its final `ei`. SMSlib helpers such as `SMS_VRAMmemcpy` re-enable them, which let a line interrupt hit the middle of the display-off screen draw and drop name-table cells (black tiles) and, later, corrupt VDP registers (the player sprite turned into font letters). The frame handler in `game_screen.c` therefore uses plain port writes only. - `dev/crt0_sms.s` is the devkitSMS startup file with one change: it clears work RAM (`$C000-$DEEE`). The stock file clears only `l__DATA` bytes from `$C000`, which missed SMSlib's variables (handler pointers) once `--data-loc` moved to `$C620`. That worked on emulators that zero RAM but halted on Emulicious and would on hardware. Check with a random-RAM start when changing startup code. - ROM addresses used as literals (`$2B49`, `$2C0B`, and the inline enemy animation tables such as `$1FD6`, listed in `DATA_POINTERS`) are rewritten to symbols by the converter, since the code is relocated. Leaving them as numbers made enemies read random ROM bytes and hung the game. - `ihx2sms` warns when the ROM exceeds 32 KB; check the `_GSINIT` area in `build/transbot.map` ends below `$7FF0`, which `tests/test_rom_assets.py` also checks. - `make test` also checks the stage streams inside the built ROM against the original and runs the original decoders and scroll setup in `sz80`.
